## GHSA-jqmf-mx4f-hfr6 · vibe-trading-ai

2026-10-02 · Python · **CRITICAL** 10.0 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTH→ENDPOINT`

### Root cause

The application had a 'dev mode' where authentication was skipped if the API_AUTH_KEY environment variable was not set. This dev mode was not sufficiently restricted to local clients, allowing remote attackers to bypass authentication entirely if the key was unset. Additionally, several sensitive API endpoints lacked explicit authentication dependencies.

### Impact

An unauthenticated remote attacker could access sensitive API endpoints, potentially leading to command execution, code injection, or Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF) if the LLM-callable tools were enabled, or at minimum, information disclosure.

### Key diff

```
--- a/agent/api_server.py
+++ b/agent/api_server.py
@@ -286,23 +289,74 @@ def _configured_api_key() -> str:
 
 
 async def require_auth(
-    cred: HTTPAuthorizationCredentials = Security(_security),
+    request: Request,
+    cred: Optional[HTTPAuthorizationCredentials] = Security(_security),
 ) -> None:
-    """Validate Bearer token against API_AUTH_KEY environment variable.
-    if not api_key:
-        return
-    if not cred or cred.credentials != api_key:
+    api_key = _configured_api_key()
+    if not api_key:
+        if _is_local_client(request):
+            return
+        raise HTTPException(
+            status_code=status.HTTP_403_FORBIDDEN,
+            detail="API_AUTH_KEY is required for non-local API access",
+        )
+
+    token = _auth_credential_from_header_or_query(cred, query_api_key, allow_query=allow_query)
+    if not token or not hmac.compare_digest(token, api_key):
         raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Invalid or missing API key")
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces stricter checks for dev-mode authentication, ensuring it only applies to local clients. It also explicitly adds `require_auth` dependencies to several previously unprotected API endpoints and uses `hmac.compare_digest` for secure token comparison. New environment flags were added to control shell tools and Docker loopback trust.

## GHSA-v2f8-6655-7grj · vibe-trading-ai

2026-10-02 · Python · **CRITICAL** 10.0 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTH→ENDPOINT`

### Root cause

The application's API endpoints lacked proper authentication checks, especially when the API_AUTH_KEY environment variable was not set. In 'dev mode' (API_AUTH_KEY unset), the system incorrectly allowed unauthenticated access from non-local clients, treating them as local. Additionally, the comparison of API keys was not constant-time, potentially leaking information.

### Impact

An unauthenticated attacker could access sensitive API endpoints, including those for file upload and potentially remote code execution (RCE) chains, leading to full system compromise.

### Key diff

```
--- a/agent/api_server.py
+++ b/agent/api_server.py
@@ -286,10 +289,20 @@ def _configured_api_key() -> str:
 
 async def require_auth(
-    cred: HTTPAuthorizationCredentials = Security(_security),
+    request: Request,
+    cred: Optional[HTTPAuthorizationCredentials] = Security(_security),
 ) -> None:
-    api_key = _configured_api_key()
-    if not api_key:
-        return
-    if not cred or cred.credentials != api_key:
+    _validate_api_auth(request=request, cred=cred)
+
+def _validate_api_auth(
+    *, request: Request, cred: Optional[HTTPAuthorizationCredentials],
+    query_api_key: Optional[str] = None, allow_query: bool = False,
+) -> None:
+    api_key = _configured_api_key()
+    if not api_key:
+        if _is_local_client(request):
+            return
+        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_403_FORBIDDEN, detail="API_AUTH_KEY is required for non-local API access")
+
+    token = _auth_credential_from_header_or_query(cred, query_api_key, allow_query=allow_query)
+    if not token or not hmac.compare_digest(token, api_key):
         raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Invalid or missing API key")
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces robust authentication checks, ensuring that even in 'dev mode' (API_AUTH_KEY unset), only genuinely local clients can bypass authentication. It also applies authentication to several previously unprotected GET endpoints and uses `hmac.compare_digest` for constant-time API key comparison to prevent timing attacks.

## GHSA-72qq-p3r5-f7wq · @a2ui/web_core

2026-10-02 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 9.3 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The application's `openUrl` function directly used an agent-supplied URL without validating its scheme. This allowed an attacker to provide a `javascript:` URI, which would then be executed in the context of the user's browser when `window.open` was called.

### Impact

An attacker could execute arbitrary JavaScript code in the user's browser, leading to session hijacking, data theft, or defacement of the application.

### Key diff

```
--- a/renderers/web_core/src/v0_9/basic_catalog/functions/basic_functions.ts
+++ b/renderers/web_core/src/v0_9/basic_catalog/functions/basic_functions.ts
@@ -427,7 +427,25 @@ export const PluralizeImplementation = createPluralizeImplementation();
  */
 export const OpenUrlImplementation = createFunctionImplementation(OpenUrlApi, args => {
   if (args.url && typeof window !== 'undefined' && window.open) {
-    window.open(args.url, '_blank');
+    const baseHref =
+      typeof window.location !== 'undefined' && window.location.href
+        ? window.location.href
+        : undefined;
+
+    let url: URL;
+    try {
+      url = baseHref ? new URL(args.url, baseHref) : new URL(args.url);
+    } catch (e: any) {
+      throw new A2uiExpressionError(`Invalid URL specified: ${args.url}`, 'openUrl', e);
+    }
+
+    // Strict protocol allowlist: Only HTTP and HTTPS are permitted.
+    if (url.protocol !== 'https:' && url.protocol !== 'http:') {
+      throw new A2uiExpressionError(`Unsupported URL scheme: ${url.protocol}`, 'openUrl');
+    }
+
+    // Always use noopener and noreferrer to prevent reverse tab-nabbing
+    window.open(url.href, '_blank', 'noopener,noreferrer');
   }
 });
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces strict URL validation within the `openUrl` function. It now parses the URL and explicitly checks if its protocol is either `https:` or `http:`, throwing an error for any other scheme. Additionally, it adds `noopener,noreferrer` to the `window.open` call to prevent reverse tab-nabbing.

## GHSA-h46j-26q3-rggf · headroom-ai

2026-10-02 · Python · **HIGH** 8.8 · Pattern: `CORS_MISCONFIGURATION→ORIGIN`

### Root cause

The application did not properly validate the 'Origin' header for WebSocket connections. This allowed an attacker to initiate a WebSocket connection from an arbitrary domain, bypassing same-origin policy protections.

### Impact

An attacker could trick a user into visiting a malicious website, which would then establish a WebSocket connection to the vulnerable application. This could lead to unauthorized data access or manipulation within the user's session.

### Key diff

```
--- a/headroom/proxy/handlers/openai.py
+++ b/headroom/proxy/handlers/openai.py
@@ -3577,6 +3653,16 @@ async def handle_openai_responses_ws(self, websocket: WebSocket) -> None:
         _ws_path = getattr(_ws_url_obj, "path", "") if _ws_url_obj is not None else ""
         if not _ws_path:
             _ws_path = "/v1/responses"
+        if not _is_allowed_websocket_origin(ws_headers):
+            logger.warning(
+                "event=websocket_origin_not_allowed request_id=%s session_id=%s path=%s origin=%r",
+                request_id,
+                session_id,
+                _ws_path,
+                _header_get(ws_headers, "origin"),
+            )
+            await websocket.close(code=1008, reason="origin not allowed")
+            return
         # WS sessions bypass the HTTP middleware that stamps X-Client: codex on
         # the Responses endpoint, so apply the same path-based stamp here before
         # classify_client runs (parallels server.py / should_stamp_codex_client).
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces origin validation for WebSocket connections. It checks the 'Origin' header against a list of allowed origins configured via environment variables or defaults to allowing only loopback origins if no explicit list is provided. If the origin is not allowed, the WebSocket connection is terminated.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 12 occurrences**

> → GHSA-ccq9-r5cw-5hwq

## GHSA-5fqc-mrg8-w798 · dulwich

2026-10-02 · Python · **HIGH** 8.6 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_WRITE`

### Root cause

The `filter-branch --index-filter` functionality in Dulwich created a temporary index by materializing tree entries into the current working directory. If a symlink pointing outside the repository was introduced in an ancestor commit, and then persisted across subsequent commits, a descendant commit's writes could be redirected by this symlink to an arbitrary location on the filesystem.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious repository that, when processed by `dulwich.filter_branch`, would write arbitrary files to arbitrary locations on the filesystem outside the intended work tree, leading to remote code execution or data corruption.

### Key diff

```
--- a/dulwich/filter_branch.py
+++ b/dulwich/filter_branch.py
@@ -208,16 +208,23 @@ def _apply_index_filter(self, tree_sha: ObjectID) -> ObjectID:
             tmp_index_path = tmp_index.name
 
         try:
-            # Build index from tree
-            build_index_from_tree(
-                ".",
-                tmp_index_path,
-                self.object_store,
-                tree_sha,
-                validate_path_element=validate_path_element_ntfs,
-            )
+            index = Index(tmp_index_path, read=False)
+            for entry in iter_tree_contents(self.object_store, tree_sha):
+                assert (
+                    entry.path is not None
+                    and entry.mode is not None
+                    and entry.sha is not None
+                )
+                if S_ISGITLINK(entry.mode):
+                    size = 0
+                else:
+                    size = self.object_store[entry.sha].raw_length()
+                index[entry.path] = index_entry_from_tree_entry(
+                    entry.mode, entry.sha, size
+                )
+            index.write()
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `_apply_index_filter` function to build the temporary index in memory directly from the tree entries, rather than materializing them to the filesystem. It iterates through tree contents and adds entries to the index without relying on `build_index_from_tree`, which previously wrote to the filesystem. A new helper function `index_entry_from_tree_entry` is introduced to create index entries with zeroed stat fields, suitable for in-memory index construction.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 63 occurrences**

## GHSA-8w8g-wq8h-fq33 · dulwich

2026-10-02 · Python · **HIGH** 8.6 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_WRITE`

### Root cause

The `checkout` function in Dulwich, when processing regular files, directly used `os.open` to write file content. This raw `os.open` call followed symlinks, bypassing the library's intended symlink protections. If a malicious repository contained a symlink pointing outside the work tree, the subsequent file write would occur at the symlink's target.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious Git repository that, when checked out, would write arbitrary content to any location on the file system where the user running the checkout process has write permissions. This could lead to remote code execution (e.g., by writing to Git hooks) or other system compromise.

### Key diff

```
-                        with os.fdopen(os.open(file_path, flags, mode), "wb") as f:
-                            f.write(obj.data)
+                        build_file_from_blob(obj, mode, file_path)
+                    elif stat.S_ISLNK(mode):
+                        build_file_from_blob(obj, mode, file_path)
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `checkout` function to use `build_file_from_blob` for writing both regular files and symlinks, instead of directly calling `os.open`. The `build_file_from_blob` function is designed to replace a symlink at the target path rather than following it, thereby preventing out-of-tree writes.

## GHSA-cm62-gvxx-vmxx · dulwich

2026-10-02 · Python · **HIGH** 8.6 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_WRITE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the `stash.pop` operation, when applying working tree changes, did not properly verify the integrity of intermediate directories. Specifically, the `os.path.exists` check, which was used to determine if parent directories needed creation, followed symlinks. This allowed an attacker to create a symlink in the worktree (e.g., `link` pointing to `.git/hooks`) that would redirect subsequent file writes from the stash operation outside the intended worktree, leading to arbitrary file write.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious stash entry and, by placing a symlink in the worktree, cause Dulwich to write arbitrary files to locations outside the repository's working directory. This could lead to remote code execution or other severe system compromise, depending on the target file's location and content.

### Key diff

```
--- a/dulwich/stash.py
+++ b/dulwich/stash.py
@@ -227,6 +275,11 @@ def symlink_fn(  # type: ignore[misc,unused-ignore]
             if not validate_path(tree_entry2.path, validate_path_element):
                 raise InvalidPathError(tree_entry2.path)
 
+            # Refuse writes whose leading path goes through a symlink left
+            # in the worktree (e.g. ``link`` -> ``.git/hooks``); the cache
+            # keeps the total cost to one ``lstat`` per unique directory.
+            _verify_leading_dirs(tree_entry2.path, safe_prefix, repo_path)
+
             full_path = _tree_to_fs_path(repo_path, tree_entry2.path)
 
             # Create parent directories if needed
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new function, `_verify_leading_dirs`, which is called before writing any file from the stash. This function explicitly checks all leading path components of a file to ensure none of them resolve through a symlink. It maintains a `safe_prefix` cache to optimize checks, mirroring a fix in Git for a similar vulnerability (CVE-2021-21300). If a symlink is encountered in a leading directory, an `InvalidPathError` is raised, preventing the write.

## GHSA-9q4r-4842-93vw · trigger.dev

2026-10-02 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.7 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→SQL`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from unsanitized user input being directly incorporated into SQL queries, specifically in the TSQL query compiler. An attacker could manipulate window-function names to inject malicious SQL code.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve cross-tenant data access, potentially reading, modifying, or deleting data belonging to other users or tenants within the application's database.

### Key diff

```
-    SESSION_SECRET: z.string(),
-    MAGIC_LINK_SECRET: z.string(),
+    SESSION_SECRET: z.string().min(1).refine(isNotInsecureSecret, INSECURE_SECRET_MESSAGE),
+    MAGIC_LINK_SECRET: z.string().min(1).refine(isNotInsecureSecret, INSECURE_SECRET_MESSAGE),
```

### Fix summary

The provided diff does not directly show the SQL injection fix. Instead, it focuses on hardening environment variable handling by preventing the use of known insecure default secret values and adding validation for secret lengths. It also introduces a mechanism for signing snapshot callbacks to prevent tampering.

## GHSA-4vpg-gwqq-w44c · github.com/siyuan-note/siyuan/kernel

2026-10-02 · Go · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

Multiple API endpoints in the `kernel/api/block.go` file lacked proper authorization checks for 'publish-mode' readers. This allowed unauthenticated or low-privileged users to access metadata and content of blocks that should have been restricted based on publishing access settings.

### Impact

An attacker could gain unauthorized access to sensitive block metadata and potentially content, leading to information disclosure that should only be available to authorized users or the public based on specific publishing configurations.

### Key diff

```
--- a/kernel/api/block.go
+++ b/kernel/api/block.go
@@ -95,6 +95,11 @@ func checkBlockRef(c *gin.Context) {
 		if "" != notebook && util.InvalidIDPattern(notebook, ret) {
 			return
 		}
+		if "" != notebook && !holdBlockRequest(c, ret, notebook) {
+			return
+		}
+		ids = filterBlockIDsByPublishAccess(c, ids, notebook)
+		exactIDs = filterBlockIDsByPublishAccess(c, exactIDs, notebook)
 		var err error
 		ret.Data, err = model.CheckBlockRefInBox(ids, exactIDs, notebook)
 		if err != nil{
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces `holdBlockRequest` and `isBlockPublishAccessible` checks, along with `filterBlockIDsByPublishAccess` and `CheckBlockIdAccessableByPublishAccess` functions, to enforce publish-access filtering on various block-related API endpoints. This ensures that only authorized blocks are accessible to readers in publish mode.

## GHSA-5rmq-chc7-m22f · vibe-trading-ai

2026-10-02 · Python · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTH→ENDPOINT`

### Root cause

The application allowed access to sensitive file-reading endpoints without proper authentication when the `API_AUTH_KEY` environment variable was not set. In this 'dev mode', the application incorrectly assumed that all requests originated from a local client, thus bypassing any authentication checks for endpoints like `/runs/{run_id}/code` and `/runs/{run_id}/pine`.

### Impact

An unauthenticated attacker could read arbitrary files from the server's file system by making requests to the `/runs/{run_id}/code` and `/runs/{run_id}/pine` endpoints, potentially leading to sensitive information disclosure or further system compromise.

### Key diff

```
--- a/agent/api_server.py
+++ b/agent/api_server.py
@@ -745,7 +852,7 @@ def _build_response_from_run_dir(run_dir: Path, elapsed: float, *, include_analy
 # API Endpoints
 # ============================================================================
 
-@app.get("/runs/{run_id}/code")
+@app.get("/runs/{run_id}/code", dependencies=[Depends(require_auth)])
 async def get_run_code(run_id: str):
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces explicit authentication requirements for several sensitive GET endpoints by adding `dependencies=[Depends(require_auth)]`. It also refines the `_is_local_client` check and strengthens the 'dev mode' authentication bypass to only allow local clients when `API_AUTH_KEY` is not set, preventing remote unauthenticated access.

## GHSA-8ffr-xgwf-xj56 · aws-smithy-json

2026-10-02 · RUST · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The `skip_inner` function, responsible for skipping unknown JSON values, used recursion to handle nested structures. An attacker could send a deeply nested JSON payload, causing the recursive calls to exceed the default thread stack size, leading to a stack overflow and application crash.

### Impact

An unauthenticated remote attacker could send a specially crafted JSON payload to a server using `aws-smithy-json`, causing the server process to crash and resulting in a denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/rust-runtime/aws-smithy-json/src/deserialize/token.rs
+++ b/rust-runtime/aws-smithy-json/src/deserialize/token.rs
@@ -358,21 +358,36 @@ pub fn skip_to_end<'a>(
     skip_inner(1, tokens)
 }
 
+/// Maximum nesting depth allowed while skipping a JSON value.
+const MAX_SKIP_DEPTH: usize = 512;
+
 fn skip_inner<'a>(
-    depth: isize,
+    initial_depth: usize,
     tokens: &mut impl Iterator<Item = Result<Token<'a>, Error>>,
 ) -> Result<(), Error> {
+    let mut depth = initial_depth;
     loop {
         match tokens.next().transpose()? {
             Some(Token::StartObject { .. }) | Some(Token::StartArray { .. }) => {
-                skip_inner(depth + 1, tokens)?;
-                if depth == 0 {
-                    break;
+                depth = depth.checked_add(1).ok_or_else(|| {
+                    Error::custom("exceeded max recursion depth while skipping value")
+                })?;
+                if depth > MAX_SKIP_DEPTH {
+                    return Err(Error::custom(
+                        "exceeded max recursion depth while skipping value"
+                    ));
                 }
             }
             Some(Token::EndObject { .. }) | Some(Token::EndArray { .. }) => {
                 debug_assert!(depth > 0);
-                break;
+                // The token iterator validates matching braces, so reaching
+                // this branch with depth == 0 indicates a bug upstream rather
+                // than untrusted input. Saturate to avoid underflow in
+                // release builds.
+                depth = depth.saturating_sub(1);
+                if depth == 0 {
+                    break;
                 }
             }
```

### Fix summary

The `skip_inner` function was refactored from a recursive implementation to an iterative one. A maximum nesting depth (`MAX_SKIP_DEPTH`) of 512 was introduced, and the function now explicitly checks and returns an error if this depth is exceeded, preventing stack overflows.

## GHSA-cjcg-cxmh-9wcr · praxis-proxy

2026-10-02 · RUST · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The application's HTTP/2 server implementation, specifically the `h2` crate, lacked explicit limits on header list sizes and concurrent streams. This allowed an attacker to send a small, compressed HTTP/2 request that, when decompressed and processed, would allocate hundreds of megabytes of memory on the server, leading to resource exhaustion.

### Impact

An attacker could trigger a denial of service by sending specially crafted HTTP/2 requests, causing the server to consume excessive memory and potentially crash or become unresponsive to legitimate requests.

### Key diff

```
--- a/protocol/src/http/pingora/handler/mod.rs
+++ b/protocol/src/http/pingora/handler/mod.rs
@@ -151,6 +154,7 @@ where
     let service_name = format!("http-proxy:{name}", name = listener.name);
     let mut proxy = http_proxy(&server.configuration, handler);
     proxy.server_options = Some(h2c_server_options());
+    proxy.h2_options = Some(h2_server_options());
     let mut service = Service::new(service_name, proxy);
     if let Some(tx) = super::listener::add_listener(&mut service, listener)? {
         cert_watcher_shutdowns.push(tx);
@@ -400,3 +404,11 @@ fn h2c_server_options() -> HttpServerOptions {
 fn h2_server_options() -> H2Options {
     let mut opts = H2Options::new();
     opts.max_header_list_size(65_536); // 64 KiB
     opts.max_concurrent_streams(128);
     opts
}
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces explicit limits for HTTP/2 options. It sets a maximum header list size to 64 KiB and a maximum of 128 concurrent streams by configuring `H2Options` for the HTTP/2 server. This prevents HPACK amplification attacks and mitigates resource exhaustion.

## GHSA-gjj5-9665-rwrc · probe-image-size

2026-10-02 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The previous SVG parsing logic used multiple regular expressions that were not anchored and could lead to quadratic time complexity on specially crafted SVG inputs. This allowed an attacker to provide an SVG file that would cause the parser to consume excessive CPU resources, leading to a denial of service.

### Impact

An attacker could provide a malicious SVG file to a service using this library, causing the service to become unresponsive or crash due to excessive CPU usage, thereby denying service to legitimate users.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/parse_sync/svg.js
+++ b/lib/parse_sync/svg.js
-var SVG_HEADER_RE = /<[-_.:a-zA-Z0-9][^>]*>/
-var SVG_TAG_RE = /^<([-_.:a-zA-Z0-9]+:)?svg\s/
-var SVG_WIDTH_RE = /[^-]\bwidth="([^%]+?)"|[^-]\bwidth='([^%]+?)'/
+var parseSvgHeader = require('../common/svg_header')
```

### Fix summary

The patch refactors the SVG parsing by introducing a new module `svg_header.js` which uses carefully crafted, anchored regular expressions with disjoint prefixes. This ensures that the parsing process remains linear even on malformed input, preventing quadratic time complexity issues.

## GHSA-x8mw-p69m-v3mx · @fastify/busboy

2026-10-02 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `PROTOTYPE_POLLUTION→OVERRIDE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from the use of plain object literals (`{}`) for `this.header` in `HeaderParser.js`. When processing multipart part headers, an attacker could send a header with a prototype-named key (e.g., '__proto__'). This would allow them to inject properties into the `Object.prototype`, which could lead to unexpected behavior or errors when other parts of the application access these properties.

### Impact

An attacker could trigger a Denial of Service by causing the application to crash or enter an unstable state due to prototype pollution, making the service unavailable to legitimate users.

### Key diff

```
--- a/deps/dicer/lib/HeaderParser.js
+++ b/deps/dicer/lib/HeaderParser.js
@@ -17,7 +17,7 @@ function HeaderParser (cfg) {
   this.maxHeaderPairs = getLimit(cfg, 'maxHeaderPairs', 2000)
   this.maxHeaderSize = getLimit(cfg, 'maxHeaderSize', 80 * 1024)
   this.buffer = ''
-  this.header = {}
+  this.header = Object.create(null)
   this.finished = false
   this.tail = ''
 }
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the vulnerability by changing the initialization of `this.header` from a plain object literal (`{}`) to `Object.create(null)`. This creates an object without a prototype, preventing an attacker from injecting properties into `Object.prototype` via specially crafted header names.

## GHSA-xjh9-v7x6-24jw · @fastify/busboy

2026-10-02 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→INFINITE_LOOP`

### Root cause

The `_occ` array, used for the Boyer-Moore string search algorithm's occurrence table, was initialized as a `Uint8Array`. This limited the maximum value an element could hold to 255. When processing an oversized multipart boundary (needle), the `needleLength` could exceed 255, leading to an integer overflow when assigned to `_occ` elements, causing incorrect search logic.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious multipart boundary with a length greater than 255. This would cause the `_occ` table to be incorrectly populated, leading to an infinite loop or highly inefficient processing during string search, ultimately resulting in a Denial of Service for the application.

### Key diff

```
--- a/deps/streamsearch/sbmh.js
+++ b/deps/streamsearch/sbmh.js
@@ -53,7 +53,7 @@ function SBMH (needle) {
   this.maxMatches = Infinity
   this.matches = 0
 
-  this._occ = new Uint8Array(256)
+  this._occ = new Uint16Array(256)
     .fill(needleLength) // Initialize occurrence table.
   this._lookbehind_size = 0
   this._needle = needle
```

### Fix summary

The patch changes the `_occ` array from `Uint8Array` to `Uint16Array`. This increases the maximum value an element can hold from 255 to 65535, preventing integer overflow when `needleLength` exceeds 255 and ensuring the occurrence table is correctly initialized for larger boundary strings.

## GHSA-35mr-4567-66vg · dulwich

2026-10-02 · Python · **MODERATE** 6.5 · Pattern: `DOS→INFINITE_LOOP`

### Root cause

The Dulwich library did not validate the 'delta_base_offset' field in OFS_DELTA pack entries. A zero value for this offset would cause the 'Pack.resolve_object' method to attempt to resolve an object against itself, leading to an infinite loop.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious packfile with a self-referencing OFS_DELTA entry. When processed by a Dulwich application, this would cause an infinite loop, consuming all available memory and leading to a denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/dulwich/pack.py
+++ b/dulwich/pack.py
@@ -1485,6 +1485,11 @@ def unpack_object(
             delta_base_offset += 1
             delta_base_offset <<= 7
             delta_base_offset += byte & 0x7F
+        if delta_base_offset == 0:
+            # A zero offset makes the delta reference itself, which would
+            # loop forever in resolve_object. git's C client rejects this
+            # with "delta offset == 0 is invalid".
+            raise ApplyDeltaError("OFS_DELTA has delta_base_offset of 0")
         delta_base = delta_base_offset
     elif type_num == REF_DELTA:
         # Determine hash size from hash_func
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds a check in the 'unpack_object' function to explicitly reject OFS_DELTA pack entries where 'delta_base_offset' is zero. If such an entry is encountered, an 'ApplyDeltaError' is now raised, preventing the infinite loop.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 21 occurrences**
> Languages: JavaScript, Python, Java, Erlang, RUST, PHP

> → GHSA-grgv-6hw6-v9g4

## GHSA-62ch-8vmq-8xm7 · figlet

2026-10-02 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→INFINITE_LOOP`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the `whitespaceBreak` logic in the `figlet` library could enter an unbounded loop. When a single character was wider than the requested output width, the `getBreakPoint` function would fail to find a suitable break point, leading to a `while` loop in `generateFigTextLines` spinning indefinitely without making progress.

### Impact

An attacker could provide specific input text and width options that would cause the `figlet` process to enter an infinite loop, consuming CPU resources and leading to a denial of service for applications using the library.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/figlet.ts
+++ b/src/figlet.ts
@@ -804,6 +804,16 @@ const figlet: FigletModule = (() => {
         };
       }
     }
+    // No break point fits within opts.width, which happens when a single
+    // character is wider than the requested width. Emit the first character
+    // on its own line so the caller always makes forward progress; otherwise
+    // the while-loop in generateFigTextLines would spin forever.
+    if (figChars.length > 0) {
+      return {
+        outputFigText: joinFigArray([figChars[0]], len, opts),
+        chars: figChars.slice(1),
+      };
+    }
     return { outputFigText: newFigChar(len), chars: figChars };
   }
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the issue by adding a check in `getBreakPoint`. If no suitable break point is found (meaning a character is wider than the requested width), it forces the first character to be emitted on its own line. This ensures forward progress in the `generateFigTextLines` loop, preventing the infinite loop. Additionally, it adds validation for font header values to prevent negative dimensions.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 21 occurrences**
> Languages: JavaScript, Python, Java, Erlang, RUST, PHP
> → GHSA-4rc3-7j7w-m548
> → GHSA-grgv-6hw6-v9g4
> → GHSA-rwm7-x88c-3g2p

## GHSA-c9xm-49cp-xcr9 · rmcp

2026-10-02 · RUST · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `SSRF→CLOUD_METADATA`

### Root cause

The rmcp OAuth client was fetching `resource_metadata` URLs provided by the authorization server without sufficient validation. This allowed an attacker to control the URL that the client would fetch, potentially directing it to internal network resources or cloud metadata endpoints.

### Impact

An attacker could leverage this vulnerability to perform Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF) attacks, accessing internal network services, cloud metadata APIs, or other sensitive resources that the vulnerable application could reach.

### Key diff

```
--- a/crates/rmcp/src/transport/auth.rs
+++ b/crates/rmcp/src/transport/auth.rs
@@ -821,6 +828,100 @@ fn is_https_url(value: &str) -> bool {
 }
 
 impl AuthorizationManager {
+    fn is_http_url(url: &Url) -> bool {
+        matches!(url.scheme(), "http" | "https") && url.host_str().is_some()
+    }
+
+    fn is_same_origin(base: &Url, candidate: &Url) -> bool {
+        base.scheme() == candidate.scheme()
+            && base
+                .host_str()
+                .zip(candidate.host_str())
+                .is_some_and(|(base, candidate)| base.eq_ignore_ascii_case(candidate))
+            && base.port_or_known_default() == candidate.port_or_known_default()
+    }
+
+    fn is_same_origin_resource_metadata_url(base_url: &Url, candidate: &Url) -> bool {
+        Self::is_http_url(candidate) && Self::is_same_origin(base_url, candidate)
+    }
+
+    fn is_disallowed_metadata_ipv4(addr: Ipv4Addr) -> bool {
+        let octets = addr.octets();
+        addr.is_private()
+            || addr.is_loopback()
+            || addr.is_link_local()
+            || addr.is_broadcast()
+            || addr.is_unspecified()
+            || addr.is_multicast()
+            || octets[0] == 0
+            || (octets[0] == 100 && (64..=127).contains(&octets[1]))
+            || (octets[0] == 198 && matches!(octets[1], 18 | 19))
+    }
+
+    fn is_disallowed_metadata_ipv6(addr: Ipv6Addr) -> bool {
+        if let Some(mapped) = addr.to_ipv4_mapped() {
+            return Self::is_disallowed_metadata_ipv4(mapped);
+        }
+
+        let segments = addr.segments();
+        addr.is_loopback()
+            || addr.is_unspecified()
+            || addr.is_multicast()
+            || (segments[0] & 0xffc0) == 0xfe80
+            || (segments[0] & 0xfe00) == 0xfc00
+    }
+
+    fn is_disallowed_metadata_hostname(host: &str) -> bool {
+        matches!(host, "localhost")
+            || host.ends_with(".localhost")
+            || CLOUD_METADATA_HOSTS.contains(&host)
+    }
+
+    fn is_disallowed_metadata_host(host: &str) -> bool {
+        let host = host.trim_end_matches('.').to_ascii_lowercase();
+        if Self::is_disallowed_metadata_hostname(&host) {
+            return true;
+        }
+
+        match host.parse::<IpAddr>() {
+            Ok(IpAddr::V4(addr)) => Self::is_disallowed_metadata_ipv4(addr),
+            Ok(IpAddr::V6(addr)) => Self::is_disallowed_metadata_ipv6(addr),
+            Err(_) => false,
+        }
+    }
+
+    fn is_allowed_authorization_server_metadata_url(url: &Url) -> bool {
+        Self::is_http_url(url)
+            && url
+                .host_str()
+                .is_some_and(|host| !Self::is_disallowed_metadata_host(host))
+    }
+
+    fn resolve_resource_metadata_url(value: &str, base_url: &Url) -> Option<Url> {
+        let value = value.trim();
+        if value.is_empty() {
+            debug!("ignoring empty resource_metadata value");
+            return None;
+        }
+
+        let url = match Url::parse(value).or_else(|_| base_url.join(value)) {
+            Ok(url) => url,
+            Err(error) => {
+                debug!("failed to parse resource metadata value `{value}` as URL: {error}");
+                return None;
+            }
+        };
+
+        if Self::is_same_origin_resource_metadata_url(base_url, &url) {
+            Some(url)
+        } else {
+            warn!(
+                "rejecting resource metadata URL `{url}` because it is not same-origin with `{base_url}`"
+            );
+            None
+        }
+    }
+
     fn well_known_paths(base_path: &str, resource: &str) -> Vec<String> {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces strict validation for `resource_metadata` URLs and authorization server metadata URLs. It now checks for same-origin policies, disallows access to known cloud metadata hosts, and prevents redirects to non-same-origin URLs during discovery, mitigating SSRF risks.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 4 occurrences**
> Languages: Python, Go, RUST
> → GHSA-fqvv-jvhr-g5jc
> → GHSA-87fv-vqqr-m4jr
> → GHSA-h47f-gmjp-m7rr

## GHSA-gg6r-gp4c-89hp · trigger.dev

2026-10-02 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTH→ENDPOINT`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from the `V1 coordinator` component in Trigger.dev, which exposed a Socket.IO endpoint without proper authentication. This allowed unauthorized access to internal services.

### Impact

An attacker could connect to the exposed Socket.IO endpoint and potentially interact with or control internal services, leading to unauthorized actions or data manipulation within the Trigger.dev environment.

### Key diff

```
--- a/apps/webapp/app/v3/marqs/index.server.ts
+++ /dev/null
@@ -1,2691 +0,0 @@
-import { type RedisOptions } from "@internal/redis";
-import type { Span, SpanOptions, Tracer } from "@opentelemetry/api";
-import { context, propagation, SpanKind, SpanStatusCode, trace } from "@opentelemetry/api";
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the vulnerability by completely removing the `apps/webapp/app/v3/marqs/index.server.ts` file, which contained the vulnerable V1 coordinator code. This eliminates the unauthenticated Socket.IO endpoint.

## GHSA-xm28-xvqc-gxxg · eu.copernik:copernik-xml-factory

2026-10-02 · Java · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `XML_EXTERNAL_ENTITY→FILE_READ`

### Root cause

The Stock JDK's XInclude processor did not properly restrict resource resolution when processing 'xi:include' hrefs. While JAXP's ACCESS_EXTERNAL_* attributes were set, the XInclude processor ignored these security features, allowing external entities to be resolved without proper validation.

### Impact

An attacker could craft an XML document with malicious XInclude elements to read arbitrary files from the server, potentially leading to information disclosure or other system compromises.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/main/java/eu/copernik/xml/factory/StockJdkProvider.java
+++ b/src/main/java/eu/copernik/xml/factory/StockJdkProvider.java
@@ -70,7 +100,9 @@ static DocumentBuilderFactory configure(final DocumentBuilderFactory factory) {
         // Defense-in-depth: already FSP-secure defaults, set explicitly so they are not relaxed via system property.
         setAttribute(factory, XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_DTD, "");
         setAttribute(factory, XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_SCHEMA, "");
-        return factory;
+        // Required: Stock JDK XInclude processor ignores ACCESS_EXTERNAL_*; a deny-all EntityResolver on every
+        // DocumentBuilder is the only way to block xi:include href resolution. Callers can override it to allow-list.
+        return new HardeningDocumentBuilderFactory(factory, new Resolvers.FallbackDenyResolver(null));
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `HardeningDocumentBuilderFactory` and explicitly sets a deny-all `EntityResolver` on `DocumentBuilder` and `XMLReader` instances. This ensures that all external resource lookups initiated by XInclude are blocked by default, even if `setXIncludeAware(true)` is called. Callers can override the resolver to allow-list specific trusted resources.
